- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Fiefdoms (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +15% Production and +100% Border Growth during "We Love the King Day."
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 5
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 7

        - HostileBias: 8

        - MinorCivWarBias: 7
        - DenounceWillingness: 9
        - Forgiveness: 4
        - Meanness: 9
        - Neediness: 6
        - Chattiness: 4
        - DeceptiveBias: 7
        - Rationale: Increasing WarBias to 9 and Boldness to 10 for final push against Babylon. With Pago Pago liberated and naval momentum against Babylon's coastal cities, need maximum aggression. VictoryCompetitiveness at 10 reflects win-or-lose scenario against Babylon's tech lead. Cannot allow Babylon to reach spaceship victory.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Air Supremacy (Policy)
        - Rationale: Next policy available in 27 turns. Air Supremacy (Level 3 Autocracy tenet) provides free airports in every city and +25% production when building aircraft. This is critical for the nuclear era - I need maximum air power for both nuclear delivery and conventional dominance. Free airports also provide immediate strategic mobility for my forces.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Barbarians
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Barbarians are free culture/gold/faith generators for Aztecs with Authority. Maximum hostility encourages hunting them aggressively. Clearing camps advances victory through city-state quests and Aztec bonuses.
        - UpdatedTurn: 1
        
        ### Colombo
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Colombo situation deteriorated - Ottomans at 69 influence vs my 0, Babylon has 168. With war against Iroquois consuming diplomatic resources and America controlling Bunkeya/Hanoi, cannot afford to compete here. Reducing from 50/50 to 0/-10 signals strategic withdrawal. Focus limited diplomatic capital on Veligrad (where I have emissary en route) and winning the war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 204
        
        ### Veligrad
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: Veligrad remains critical priority. Currently Friends (62 influence) with emissary en route, need 60+ for Alliance. Securing Veligrad denies Iroquois northern foothold and applies flanking pressure. Ottomans lead at 126 but distance decay may allow me to overtake. Essential for diplomatic positioning against Iroquois-American axis.
        - UpdatedTurn: 205
        
        ### Rio de Janeiro
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Rio de Janeiro has fallen to Barbarians (captured in Turn 205, resistance ongoing). City-state ally status completely lost - Ottomans replaced me as ally just before fall, then barbarians conquered. No longer viable diplomatic partner. Reducing from 100/90 to 0/-30 reflects strategic reality: Rio is now effectively enemy barbarian territory. Cannot commit resources to liberation while Brantford siege is critical priority. Treat as hostile zone if encountered.
        - UpdatedTurn: 206
        
        ### Utica
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: HANOI REALITY CHECK: America flipped Hanoi to 228 influence vs my 186. Cannot compete diplomatically while funding war. Reducing to 0/-10 signals strategic withdrawal from this city-state. Focus on Bunkeya and Veligrad instead.
        - UpdatedTurn: 281
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: MAINTAIN maximum hostility against Iroquois. Onondaga captured but Osininka remains their new capital. War score at 100% with 100% war weariness - complete capitulation imminent. No peace until Iroquois are eliminated or vassalized.
        - UpdatedTurn: 358
        
        ### Pago Pago
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Pago Pago has been defeated and puppeted by Babylon (captured Turn 387). City-state no longer exists as independent diplomatic entity. Resetting relationship to neutral/0 to reflect strategic reality - Pago Pago is now effectively Babylon territory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### Warsaw
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Warsaw - Friends (38 influence), currently under Open Door policy. Maintaining moderate positive relationship for delegate access (1 delegate from friendship). Not investing heavily due to nuclear crisis, but keeping door open for future alliance when Open Door is repealed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### Bunkeya
        - Public: 20
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Bunkeya - Japan flipped alliance (270 vs my 34), America has 162. I've lost the aluminum source. With nuclear-armed Babylon as primary threat, I cannot afford to compete diplomatically here. Demoting relationship from 0/-25 to 20/10 to acknowledge strategic reality but keep options open post-war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### The Celts
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Celts alliance maintained. They're Japan's vassal with 8 delegates, fighting Babylon (War Score -8%). Part of the anti-Babylon Autocracy coalition. Need their World Congress votes to block Babylon-sponsored resolutions and potentially pass sanctions against Babylon or France.
        - UpdatedTurn: 395
        
        ### France
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: France remains Babylon's vassal. Maximum hostility maintained - eliminating France denies Babylon vassal tribute and strategic depth. France is weak (1352 strength, 1 city) - easy target once I break through Babylon's defenses. Cannot allow Babylon to maintain economic advantages from vassalage while possessing nuclear weapons.
        - UpdatedTurn: 395
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Ottoman alliance remains the cornerstone of anti-Babylon strategy. They're fighting Babylon near Grand River with landships and infantry. With nuclear weapons becoming available, coordinating with Ottomans for a two-front nuclear-conventional assault on Babylon could be decisive. Their 6886 military strength and 20 delegates are invaluable.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: Japan made peace with Babylon but remains important. They have 25 World Congress delegates and 12228 military strength. Maintaining positive relations (90/85 down from 95/90) preserves access to their votes while acknowledging their reduced commitment to the anti-Babylon coalition. Their tourism/culture power (2447 culture/turn) also makes them a potential cultural victory threat to monitor.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Babylon
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Babylon remains existential threat with Apollo Program, atomic bombs, and 10 Uranium. Maximum hostility maintained. War score only 12% - need to push harder now that Manhattan Project is nearly complete. Their 35% happiness and 82% war weariness means they're vulnerable to continued pressure. No peace until they capitulate or are eliminated.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### America
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: America has Defensive Pact with Babylon but hasn't actively attacked me. Increasing hostility from -40 to -50 reflects their continued protection of Babylon, but not maximizing hostility allows potential future rapprochement if they abandon Babylon. Their 7159 military strength and 21 delegates make them dangerous if provoked into active war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Hanoi
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Hanoi quest to capture Buffalo Creek (32 turns remaining) offers 462 influence and a Juggernaut unit. This is valuable for both the reward and maintaining northern pressure. Maximizing relationship ensures continued quest availability and potential alliance after quest completion. Buffalo Creek is also strategically positioned for operations against Babylon.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Nuclear era warfare requires maximum air power and anti-air defense. Increasing UseNuke to 85 as Manhattan Project completes in 2 turns - need aggressive nuclear posture against Babylon. Air priority raised to 100 for aerial dominance. Naval priorities slightly reduced as focus shifts to land/air campaign against Babylon's core territories. Science remains high to reach Nuclear Fusion after Fission completes.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 45
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 7 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 395
          - Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Riga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 395
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - Sanction: France:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Colombo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 417
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - Japanese Sphere of Influence: Ur:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 417
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 406
          - Open Door: Enns:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 406
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: 393
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Open Door: Utica:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - Proposer: The Ottomans
          - Repeal: Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Riga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: America
          - Enact: International Space Station:
            - Description: Begins the International Space Station project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : International Space Station wonder appears in Capital. (+1 Production from Scientists, and +1 Science from Engineers. Great Scientists provide 33% more Science when used to discover new Technology. +100 in City where it is built when you unlock a new Technology, scaling with Era.)
              : Free Great Scientist.
              : One time Research boost.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Rocketry.
            - Proposer: Japan

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Fiefdoms (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +15% Production and +100% Border Growth during "We Love the King Day."
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - #0:

          - text: **Researching Nuclear Capabilities**
            
            My focus is on advancing our technological edge. I'm prioritizing "Nuclear Fission" research, expecting it to conclude in 11 turns. Concurrently, I'm setting our next policy to "Air Supremacy" for a future turn, aiming to solidify our dominance.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Entanglements**
            
            I'm currently reassessing our strategic position. It appears America, despite its previous "Hostile" stance towards us, has a Defensive Pact with Babylon. This complicates our ongoing conflict with Babylon, especially considering our current war footing. I need to understand the implications of this alliance.
            
            **Clarifying Alliances and Conflicts**
            
            I'm untangling the web of international relations. It seems the defensive pact America has with Babylon, combined with our existing non-aggression pact, is why we aren't directly at war with them, despite our conflict with Babylon. I need to confirm the exact nature of this pact.
            
            **Assessing War Performance**
            
            I'm reviewing our current war efforts. We maintain a 12% war score advantage against Babylon, who is suffering immense war weariness at 84% despite our own 34%. Babylon is clearly being overwhelmed by a coalition, and their military strength has plummeted.
            
            **Planning City Captures**
            
            I'm strategizing which of Babylon's cities to target next, focusing on those adjacent to our controlled territories like Onondaga and Buffalo Creek. Borsippa and Dur-Kurigalzu appear to be prime targets due to their proximity and defensive strength, with Babylon's capital also being a key objective.
            
            **Targeting Babylonian Cities**
            
            I'm refining my city capture plan. Borsippa, Sippar, and Akkad are now top priorities due to their proximity to our borders. I'm particularly interested in Nippur, where our forces have already inflicted significant damage. Our overwhelming military strength positions us well for these advances.
            
            **Capturing Capitals Strategically**
            
            I'm analyzing the implications of capturing Babylon. It appears that if I take Babylon, I'll gain control of three original capitals: Tenochtitlan, Onondaga, and Babylon itself. This raises questions about the fate of Paris, currently a vassal of Babylon, and the potential to then vassalize France.
            
            **Advancing Towards Domination**
            
            Capturing Babylon seems to align perfectly with our Conquest Grand Strategy and brings us closer to a Domination victory. I'm also examining policy and technology options, specifically focusing on whether "Air Supremacy" is readily available.
            
            **Researching Policy Tenets**
            
            I'm double-checking our policy branch to see if we meet the requirements for a Level 3 Tenet. It appears we have enough tenets in Autocracy, and "Air Supremacy" is indeed our next policy, which is excellent given its strong bonuses for aircraft production and airports.
            
            **Acquiring Fissile Material**
            
            My immediate concern is securing Uranium. Though the Manhattan Project is almost complete, we have no Uranium to utilize it. I'm investigating if we have unmined deposits or require further technological advancements to access it, as our enemies possess this critical resource.
            
            **Securing Uranium Sources**
            
            I'm confirming our Uranium situation. While Nuclear Fission research is nearing completion, we currently lack any discovered Uranium deposits within our borders. I've identified the Ottomans and Japan as potential sources through trade, given our existing diplomatic ties and their stockpiles.
            
            **Identifying Key Technologies**
            
            I'm clarifying technology dependencies. My previous thought about Nuclear Fission revealing Uranium was incorrect; Atomic Theory grants that, which we already possess. The immediate goal is to secure Uranium once Nuclear Fission is researched to leverage the Manhattan Project.
            
            **Analyzing Global Trade Routes**
            
            I'm investigating trade opportunities. The Ottomans and Japan both possess significant Uranium reserves, and our current diplomatic standing with them suggests a favorable trade environment. This is crucial for utilizing our Manhattan Project.
            
            **Evaluating Strategic Advancements**
            
            I'm reassessing our technological path. While "Combined Arms" is an available option, my priority remains securing the Uranium necessary to fully exploit the Manhattan Project and advance our nuclear capabilities.
            
            **Confirming Fission Research**
            
            I'm tracking the progress of Nuclear Fission. Its completion is imminent and will unlock the ability to utilize the Manhattan Project, provided we can secure the necessary Uranium resources.
            
            **Prioritizing Uranium Acquisition**
            
            My immediate focus is on acquiring Uranium. With Nuclear Fission research nearing completion, and the Manhattan Project nearly ready, the lack of this crucial resource is a significant bottleneck. I am exploring trade with the Ottomans and Japan.
            
            **Navigating Technological Dependencies**
            
            I'm clarifying the technology tree. My understanding was that Nuclear Fission reveals Uranium, but it's actually Atomic Theory. Since we already have Atomic Theory, the immediate next step after Nuclear Fission is securing Uranium to use the Manhattan Project.
            
            **Optimizing Resource Procurement**
            
            I'm strategizing our resource acquisition. The Ottomans and Japan are key targets for Uranium trade, given their substantial reserves and our positive diplomatic relations. This is essential for leveraging the Manhattan Project.
            
            **Selecting Next Research Path**
            
            I've identified "Combined Arms" as the optimal next research, providing a crucial advantage with Tanks and Radar, essential for our conquest strategy. This choice supersedes previous considerations, as it unlocks critical military advancements.
            
            **Confirming Policy Choice**
            
            I'm reaffirming "Air Supremacy" as our next policy. The bonuses to aircraft production and free airports are vital for our bomber-heavy military. I've confirmed its availability within the Autocracy tenets.
            
            **Analyzing Alliance Dynamics**
            
            My current concern is the defensive pact between America and Babylon. Given our ongoing war with Babylon and our previous territorial disputes with America, this alliance creates significant strategic complexities. I need to ascertain if this pact will trigger a war declaration from America.
            
            **Assessing America's Intentions**
            
            I'm analyzing America's potential involvement in our conflict. Their defensive pact with Babylon and existing friendship with the Ottomans, our allies, creates a complex diplomatic situation. I'm weighing the risks of their intervention against improving relations to deter aggression, prioritizing our conquest of Babylon.
            
            **Capturing Wonders and Weakening Defenses**
            
            I'm focusing on acquiring cities rich with wonders to boost our empire's strengths. Nippur's defenses are critically low, making its capture imminent, which will then open the path to Borsippa and Babylon. Our military presence near these targets is substantial.
            
            **Unleashing Aerial Superiority**
            
            My air force, comprising 10 Bombers and 7 Triplanes, is poised for an overwhelming assault. Coupled with 7 Artilleries, we can decimate Nippur's defenses, paving the way for the swift capture of Borsippa and Babylon.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Influences**
            
            I'm reassessing our relationships, noting Japan's significant cultural influence over us and their progress towards a cultural victory. This makes them a strategic concern for a potential domination victory.
            
            **Securing Uranium Reserves**
            
            My immediate focus remains acquiring Uranium. Nuclear Fission research is nearly complete, and I'm prioritizing trade talks with the Ottomans and Japan to secure this critical resource.
            
            **Refining City Capture Strategy**
            
            I'm prioritizing the swift capture of Nippur due to its weakened defenses, followed by Borsippa and Babylon. My air and ground forces are positioned for a decisive assault.
            
            **Optimizing Next Research Choice**
            
            I've decided to research "Combined Arms" next, as it unlocks crucial advancements like Tanks and Radar, essential for our ongoing conquest.
            
            **Confirming Policy Implementation**
            
            I'm proceeding with "Air Supremacy" as our next policy, securing valuable bonuses for aircraft production and airports to bolster our military might.
            
            **Clarifying Alliance Implications**
            
            I'm analyzing the defensive pact between America and Babylon, considering its impact on our current war and potential future conflicts.
            
            **Assessing Potential Threats**
            
            I'm evaluating America's strategic stance, weighing their alliances and potential for intervention against our immediate goals in Babylon.
            
            **Acquiring Key Wonders**
            
            I'm targeting cities with significant wonders, like Nippur, to enhance our empire's overall strength and strategic position.
            
            **Preparing for Aerial Assault**
            
            My bomber and triplane formations are ready for an overwhelming attack on Nippur, aiming to quickly secure the city and advance our objectives.
            
            **Monitoring Cultural Threats**
            
            I've identified a critical threat: Japan is dangerously close to achieving a Cultural Victory, potentially securing it within 18 turns. Their influence is rapidly expanding, requiring immediate attention to counter their victory condition.
            
            **Monitoring Cultural Victory Path**
            
            I'm analyzing Japan's rapid progression towards a Cultural Victory, which now seems a more immediate threat than Babylon's domination. Their "Passport System" resolution and our open borders deal amplify their tourism. I must close our borders and terminate our trade routes with them to slow their progress.
            
            **Ending Trade with Japan**
            
            I'm terminating our trade relationship with Japan. Their imminent Cultural Victory is a significant threat, and closing our borders and ending trade is crucial to slowing their progress, especially since they have an incoming trade route that will expire next turn.
            
            **Analyzing Victory Conditions**
            
            I'm evaluating all victory paths. Japan leads significantly in both Cultural and Diplomatic victories, now posing a more immediate threat than Babylon. I must sever ties with them, particularly closing borders and halting trade, to counter their progress and re-align our strategic priorities.
            
            **Securing Multiple Capitals**
            
            I'm prioritizing capturing Babylon, which will grant me control over three original capitals: Tenochtitlan, Onondaga, and Babylon itself. My analysis indicates I can then easily seize Paris, bringing my total to four. This makes the remaining capitals (Kyoto, Edinburgh, Istanbul, and Washington) more attainable.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Risks**
            
            I'm carefully weighing the diplomatic fallout of confronting Japan. Declaring war on them, despite their imminent cultural victory, risks severe global denouncements due to our Declaration of Friendship, potentially isolating us and jeopardizing alliances like the one with the Ottomans.
            
            **Reducing Cultural Victory Threshold**
            
            I'm realizing that by capturing France and Babylon, I significantly reduce the number of civilizations Japan needs to influence for their cultural victory. This is a critical insight for counteracting their progress.
            
            **Accelerating Japan's Victory**
            
            I've realized eliminating France and Babylon actually accelerates Japan's Cultural Victory. With fewer civilizations remaining, they need to influence fewer targets. They're already influential with the Celts and Aztecs, and becoming influential with the Ottomans is imminent.
            
            **Boosting Cultural Output**
            
            My immediate concern is countering Japan's Cultural Victory. I'm investigating ways to rapidly increase our culture output, specifically exploring if Tenochtitlan can switch from the Manhattan Project to a cultural building to slow Japan's influence and potential victory.
            
            **Boosting Culture Flavor**
            
            I've realized our low Culture flavor is a critical vulnerability to Japan's impending Cultural Victory. I must significantly increase this flavor to prioritize cultural buildings, generating much-needed culture and blocking their tourism.
            
            **Boosting Culture Flavor**
            
            My current focus is on rapidly increasing our cultural output. I've realized our low Culture flavor is a significant vulnerability against Japan's impending Cultural Victory. I will leverage our existing cultural buildings and focus on generating Great Works to bolster our culture and impede their tourism. I am also considering adjusting our Grand Strategy to better reflect our current situation.
            
            **Adjusting Strategy Focus**
            
            I'm shifting our Grand Strategy to Conquest, but with a significant short-term boost to Culture to counter Japan's imminent victory. I'm adjusting flavor priorities to reflect this, focusing on offense and production while increasing culture and espionage. I'm also prioritizing gold generation to support our extensive military.
            
            **Leveraging Gold Generation**
            
            I'm realizing our massive gold and faith income stems from our Aztec UA. Killing units provides significant gold and faith, explaining our high per-turn income despite a low net positive. Capturing cities will further boost this, and winning a war will trigger a Golden Age.
            
            **Preparing for Japan Conflict**
            
            My current focus is on preparing for a potential conflict with Japan. I'm strategically maintaining our Declaration of Friendship to avoid premature hostility while closely monitoring their cultural victory progress. I'm also investigating World Congress resolutions as a means to indirectly hinder their tourism and influence.
            
            **Voting with Allies**
            
            I'm confirming our voting strategy in the World Congress. Our AI will naturally align with the Ottomans against Japan's proposals, which perfectly suits our current diplomatic and competitive landscape. This ensures we vote against the International Space Station, for example.
            
            **Refining Conquest and Culture**
            
            My persona is set for aggressive conquest, aligning with my strategy. I've confirmed "Air Supremacy" is my next policy and "Combined Arms" the research path. I'm adjusting flavors to boost Culture and Production, and maximizing gold generation for military upkeep and potential Golden Ages.
            
            **Selecting Next Technology**
            
            I've decided to research "Combined Arms." This technology unlocks Tanks, a significant military upgrade over Landships, and also leads to Radar. I've confirmed it's the optimal choice for our conquest strategy.
            
            **Boosting Culture Defenses**
            
            I'm focusing on a critical culture defense. To counter Japan's imminent Cultural Victory, I'm significantly boosting our Culture and Great People flavors to 80 and 60 respectively. This adjustment is vital to generate necessary culture and block their tourism, while maintaining our conquest strategy.
